Three ways climb the fault: the west and east switchbacks (causeways out to buttressed ledges on the cliff's lip, the *West* and *East Fault Switchbacks*, deck Y 22 and Y 24, then on up to the *West* and *East Stormkeeps*, deck Y 30 and Y 32), and in the middle the arched *Thunder-Viaduct* from the Barbican to its pier (deck Y 20) and up the grand steps under the *Storm-Bell Gate* (Y 36). On the plateau the *Capacitor Spire* (deck Y 38) is a dead end off the West Stormkeep, and both Stormkeeps and the Storm-Bell Gate (by the keep steps) converge on the *Tempest Redoubt* (Apex, Y 48). The viaduct spans are open round arches on tall dry-stone piers with copper conduits along both faces (`Viaducts`, `TowerRoomBuilder`); the other causeways are `KAIRN_STONE`. The boss portals stand 46 studs either side of centre (`Portals.Spread`) so the citadel keep rises between them. Authored patrols: `LOWER_WALL`, `FAULT_VIADUCT`, `UPPER_REDOUBT` beside `BASIN_LOOP`; the walk to the Redoubt is under 1.5× the canonical floor's. Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/ThunderFloor29.luau`; fixed footprints checked by the specs): the citadel keep behind the Redoubt (about 163 studs), crowned by a copper storm spire with four nested rings and sky lightning forking onto its tip; the Capacitor Spire (nested copper coils round a caged violet nexus, arcs and static), braided copper cables from it and the two southern Stormkeep towers to the keep's spire; the Storm-Bell Gate astride the viaduct's steps with its giant verdigris bell 23 studs over the walk; the barbican gatehouse (twin drum towers, raised portcullis, war banners) at the causeway's mouth; round Stormkeep towers on the bastions' outer corners, lightning-spear ballistae trained over the fault, the Crag Channeler's forked-bolt dais beside the gate; crenellated curtain walls round every ward, broken wherever a path crosses; the great copper signal horns on both Horn Posts; thunder-ram stables in the East Ram Yard and a sheep fold in the West; copper-caged nexus braziers along the Outer Causeway; storm-water falls down the scarp, copper conduits climbing it beside the viaduct and retaining buttresses under both switchbacks; mist banks and violet underglow in the chasm; a half-toppled watchtower on the western cliff, far relay spires on the peaks, war banners at the bridgeheads, pines, cairns and stormgrass. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_thunder_f29_kit.py` (13 templates, each with a part-built fallback); grade: electric dusk (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[29]`).
  - **Floor 30, Roc's Eyrie (`thunder:5`, Biome Boss: Storm Roc):** the summit caldera at the roof of the Thunder Highlands, wrapped around an open swirling Cloud-Eye vortex (`795 × 795 × 290`, open sky, `Bottom = -8`, `CLOUD_EYE` + `SEA_STACK_APRON` + `LIGHTNING_SCAR` + `APEX_ALCOVE` terrain; guide image `reference/Thunder Highlands 30.jpg`). 16 terraces, 19 links and 15 walk heights (strictly beating Floor 29 and Floors 5, 10, 15, 20 and 25; 35 live camp mobs, 4 Elites, plus the **Storm Roc** Biome Boss summoned on the flat dark-slate `180 × 132` *Roc's Eyrie* dais at Y 56, `95`-stud leash, two chasm return ramps). **Shape: a Twin-Wing Caldera Amphitheatre around an open Cloud Eye.** From *Thunderhead Threshold* (Basin, Y 0), the *Eye-Rim Overlook* (Crossing, Y 12) projects a hammered-copper-coped stone causeway (`CopperCoping`) straight out onto the cantilevered circular *Storm Eye Pulpit* (deck Y 16) suspended over the cloud vortex — with **no path across the Cloud Eye to the summit** (`Crossing_Apex` and `EyePulpit_Apex` absent), so every ascent curves up either wing of the amphitheatre. The **West Wing** climbs via six Kairnfolk rope-and-copper suspension skybridges (`Skybridges` per-link override in `TowerRoomBuilder`) across *West Feather Shelf* (Y 8), *West Talon Perch* (dead-end deck Y 14), *West Pinion Crag* (Y 20), *West Gale Bastion* (Y 32), *Shattered Crown Spire* (dead-end Y 40) and *West Aerie Landing* (Y 44); the **East Wing** climbs via fortified `KAIRN_STONE` crenellated dry-stone causeways and decks across *East Feather Shelf* (deck Y 10), *East Talon Perch* (dead-end deck Y 18), *East Pinion Crag* (deck Y 22), *East Gale Bastion* (deck Y 34) and *East Aerie Landing* (deck Y 46), before twin grand slate stairs (`STAIRS`, +12/+10) ascend into *Roc's Eyrie* (deck Y 56). Boss portals stand wide at `±66` (`Portals.Spread`) so the colossal spire rises cleanly between them. Authored patrols (`WEST_WING`, `EAST_WING`, `EYE_CHORD`, `EYRIE_CROWN` plus `BASIN_LOOP`) replace generic routes. Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/ThunderFloor30.luau`, 31 spec-checked footprints): the **204.9-stud** twin-peak *Shattered Sky-Spire* with crackling chasm lightning framed by seven curved copper-clad *Lightning Crown Arches* behind the dais (never overhanging the boss arena or portals); the 16-segment *Woven Great Nest Rim* (storm-blasted pine trunks, braided rope lashings and drum banners) wrapping *Roc's Eyrie* outside its combat floor; the circular *Storm Eye Pulpit* balustrade and drum sleeve; six sweeping catenary *Hanging Abyss Chains* with tattered Kairnfolk banners draped across the 84-bank swirling *Cloud-Eye Vortex* (with 3 invisible lilac-violet underglow anchors and mist swirls); foreground *Threshold Ruin Arch* and *Observatory Ruin*; sea-stack *Gale-Lantern Towers* and gabled highland *Observatory Hall*; twin *Wind-Harp Towers*, *Shattered Crown Fragment*, *Aerie Threshold Totems*, impaled *Colossal Storm-Roc Feathers*, *Feather-Heart Shrine*, bleached *Caldera Rib Bones*, fulgurite glass spires, three roaring cliff waterfalls plunging into the cloud sea, crenellated dry-stone curtain walls, eight outer caldera lightning rods on slate plinths, sea-stack skyline crags, supercell rain, wind-bent pines, cairns and stormgrass. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_thunder_f30_kit.py` (13 templates, each with a part-built fallback); grade: Eye of the Maelstrom dramatic silver-violet calm with warm-gold cliff rim bounce (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[30]`).
  - **Floor 31, Blighted Verge (`corrupted:1`):** the edge of the Corrupted Grove, where the **Hollin** held the line. They were grove-wardens and grafters who grafted every tree of the forest onto one trunk, the Mother Graft; it rotted from the heart (the **Heartrot**) and woke as the Blight Sovereign (Floor 35). Their sigil is the graft-knot (two branches crossed and bound by a ring), and the player follows their **Pale Bands**, the linen bands they tied round every tree they saved, up all five floors (plan: `docs/prompts/corrupted-grove-floor-layouts-prompt.md`; guide image `reference/Corrupted Grove 31.webp`). `Size = { Width = 540, Depth = 580, Height = 195 }`: 11 terraces, 13 links and 10 walk heights, against 9 / 9 / 8 at best on Floors 1, 6, 11, 16 and 21. **Shape: a meandering blight-brook.** It rises at the *Heartrot Spring* (a split elder east of the Apex, its rot-heart glowing magenta) and winds west to a sinkhole, splitting the floor into an autumn-gold south bank and a rotten north bank. The Apex (*Heartrot Spring*, Y 28) sits in the far north-east corner, in view across the water from the *Warden's Stile*. Four **fords** cross the brook, and every route to the Apex uses at least one (`Fords`; the spec checks that without them the Apex is out of reach): the *Stepping-Stone Ford* and the *Weir Ford* are earthen causeways, the *Mill-Race Ford* a hung plank walkway and the *Fallen-Log Ford* a colossal fallen trunk (`Terrain.BridgeStyles = { LOG }`). The quick way fords once (*The Last Green*, *Lantern Copse*, Weir Ford), the zigzag three times (*Pale Glade*, *Weir Mill*, *Lantern Copse*, *Rothollow*), and a back path along the rotten bank (*Warding Shrine*, *The Blight Line*) reaches the Apex from the west; the *Rootstep Stairs* climb from Rothollow. Terrain: the Fungal `Terrain` system with the new `HOLLIN` surface (pale sand trails over brown earth, slate cliffs; mud only under the water, since its ripples read as water on dry ground), the brook a channel 1.5 studs below its banks (`LiquidRise = -1.6`), seven low hillocks, and `GroveScale = 0` / `MeadowScale = 0.15` so the floor places its own trees. `PathStyle = "HOLLIN"`: fieldstone treads and pavers, per-deck looks (`Terrain.DeckLooks`), no vault lanterns. Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/CorruptedGrove.luau`, 11 spec-checked footprints): the Heartrot Spring elder (113.6 studs), the thatched mill and its wheel on the brook, a fieldstone weir with foam, banded golden beeches in the Pale Glade and the Lantern Copse with wicker lanterns, the great oak on the Last Green, the wardens' lean-to, fire ring and sawn stumps at the Woodcutters' Ring, the Warding Shrine cairn, the felled Blight Line log hung with torn bands, the sinkhole, the woven stile arch over the arrival, groves on both banks and the wall slopes, rot-lilies and reeds along the brook, and golden grass, bracken, rot-blooms and blight pods as ground cover. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_corrupted_f31_kit.py` (15 templates); grade: a sickly gold late afternoon with a rose haze, the least murky Corrupted floor (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[31]`).
  - **Floor 32, Twisted Orchard (`corrupted:2`):** the Hollin's grafting orchard (`620 × 640 × 220`, `Bottom = 0`; guide image `reference/Corrupted Grove 32.jpg`). 13 terraces, 15 links and 12 walk heights (strictly beating Floor 31 and Floors 2, 7, 12, 17 and 22; 22 live camp mobs and 2 Elites). **Shape: up and over.** Every route climbs a continuous south-facing terraced orchard slope (`Terrain.Slope`) from the *Orchard Lawn* (Basin, Y 0) up to the unbroken E–W **Graft Ridge** (`Terrain.Ridges`: *Crookback Ridge* Y 40, *The Grafted Crown* Y 48, *Thorn Saddle* Y 44) and then **descends** behind the crest into the hidden *Pressing Hollow* (Apex, Y 36, lower than the crest and completely hidden from the Sanctuary arrival): centre up the *Grafters' Stair* (`STAIRS`, +32, under a living flowering pleached tunnel) to the Grafted Crown and down the *Press Stair* (`STAIRS`, −12); west through *West Rows* → *West Espalier* → *Crookback Ridge* (with a ridge-walk joining Crookback Ridge to the Crown); east through *East Rows* → *Skep Terrace* → *Thorn Saddle* (with the *Cider Vats* dead-end spur). *Windfall Hollow* (Y 6) is a built slatted timber fruit-drying deck (`Terrain.Decks`, `DeckLooks`). Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/CorruptedFloor32.luau`, 20 spec-checked footprints): the **130.5-stud** *Great Cider Press* and broad woven-willow *Press-House Roof* on the hollow's back-west rim pouring glowing magenta must with dark press-house smoke rising above the ridge; the five-species *Grafted Crown* tree on the ridge crest (pink blossom, golden autumn, olive fruiting, plum-crimson, pale white blossom plus one bare dead eastern limb, every living limb banded); the flowering *Orchard Gate* and four-arch *Grafters' Stair* tunnel; *Windfall Hollow*'s heaped fruit piles and drying arbor; the *Grafters' Yard* workbenches with terracotta rootstock pots and bubbling wax cauldrons; the mossy fieldstone *West Espalier* walls; the *Skep Terrace* benches of woven straw bee-skeps with humming glowing hives; the burst cooperage *Cider Vat* spilling magenta must down the eastern slope beside the *Felled Row*'s stumps and smouldering brash; *Crookback Ridge*'s hunched pear tree over a carved Hollin bench; *Thorn Saddle*'s dark briar arches; and E–W terraced orchard rows of crutch-propped grafted trees, ladders, baskets, puddles and ground cover. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_corrupted_f32_kit.py` (15 templates); grade: overripe rose dusk (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[32]`).
  - **Floor 33, Briar Labyrinth (`corrupted:3`):** the hedge-maze the Hollin wardens planted over a dry sunken thorn pit to wall in the Thornheart (`680 × 720 × 250`, `Bottom = -6`; guide image `reference/Corrupted Grove 33.jpg`). 15 terraces, 17 links and 14 walk heights (strictly beating Floor 32 and Floors 3, 8, 13, 18 and 23; 25 live camp mobs, 3 Elites, 2 pit return ramps, and `ReplacePatrols` with `GATE_WALK`, `WEST_MAZE` and `OUTER_RING`). **Shape: a hedge-walled labyrinth around a centred Apex.** *Thornheart Court* (Apex, Y 44) stands in the exact centre of the room, visible over the low hedges (≤ 9 studs) from *Hedge Stile* (Sanctuary) and *Briar Lawn* (Basin, Y 0), with the overgrown *Briar Gate* (Crossing, Y 8) directly in front and **no path across the pit** (`Crossing_Apex` absent). Terrain uses the deeply corrupted `Surfaces.BLIGHT` palette and `Terrain.Blight` relief (violet cobble trails, indigo blighted earth, blackened basalt cliffs/fissures, jagged pit crags and glowing cliff/pit corruption veins; used from Floor 33 onwards). Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/CorruptedFloor33.luau`, 19 spec-checked footprints): the **153.5-stud** *Thornheart* knot and split blackened elder stump with its blazing magenta cavity on a northern pit mound behind the Court; the overgrown *Briar Gate* doors; the Mender's thatched *Wattle Croft*, bench and scarecrow; the fallen warden's briar nest at *Thornwrack*; the *Hanging Garden* planter pergola; the hacked *Nettle Turn* three-arm signpost; *Briar Cloister*'s fieldstone wellhead; the *North Hedge Gate* arch framing the Mother Graft's far-north horizon silhouette; three dead gallows trees with empty wicker cages at *Gibbet Thicket*; *East Rosary*'s ruined arches draped in shield-sized rot-roses; overgrown topiary stag and Hollin graft-knot on the thorn pit floor; true-path Pale Bands and false-path unbanded hedges; and ground cover. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_corrupted_f33_kit.py` (16 templates); grade: crisp hard-shadow violet noon (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[33]`).

  - **Floor 36, Drowned Steps (`sunken:1`):** the Nereth city's sea-front, the first Sunken Sanctuary floor (plan: `docs/prompts/sunken-sanctuary-floor-layouts-prompt.md`; guide image `reference/Sunken Sanctuary 36.webp`). The **Nereth** were tide-priests who built on the seabed inside the *Stillwater Ward*, a dome of held-back sea that the Tidal Leviathan (Floor 40) held up; their sigil is the tide-spiral, and the player follows their bronze **Tidebells** up all five floors. `570 × 600 × 210`, open sky; 12 terraces, 16 links and 11 walk heights (above Floor 31 and Floors 1, 6, 11, 16, 21 and 26), the Apex at Y 32, the walk from the Sanctuary to the Apex within 2× the canonical floor's. **Shape: a broken processional stair over a tide-flat.** From the *Pilgrims' Gate* (a bell arch over the arrival) the *Tide-Flat* (Basin, Y 0) runs along the shore; the *Pilgrim Stair* climbs from the *Stair Foot* (Crossing, Y 8) up the *First Flight* (`STAIRS`) to the *Pilgrims' Landing* (a marble deck on piers, Y 16), and stops: its middle flight lies broken in the surf with its bell drowned beside it, and there is **no** link from the Landing to the *Bell Landing* (Y 24) beyond the gap (`BrokenFlight`; the spec checks the Apex is still reachable without one). Detours run round the gap east through the *Votive Pool* (a deck, Y 10, floating lamp bowls in the surf round it) and west down to the wrecked *Hollin Barge* on its sandbar (a deck, Y 6) and up *Cantors' Rock* (Y 20, a ring of lecterns); a scenic route climbs the *Tidepool Steps* (EastGallery, Y 12) to the *Upper Pools* (Y 28). The Apex, *The Bell Stack* (Y 32), is a sheer limestone sea-stack in the back-west (`Terrain.Shapes` `Sheer`), entered up the *Bell Stair* (`STAIRS`) from the south or from Cantors' Rock in the west. Four links cross open water as `Terrain.BridgeStyles = "CAUSEWAY"` (flagged limestone on round-arched piers, low parapets with marble posts). Terrain: the Fungal `Terrain` system with the new `NERETH` surface (limestone trails over sand, sandstone patches, rock banks and headlands, limestone sea-cliffs, slate pool beds), one sheet of turquoise shallows over all the open ground just under the floor's slab (`LiquidRise = -0.6`, slate pool beds) with six sandbars, and a low north wall band (`WallBand.NorthScale = 0.3`) so the Tidewall shows over it. `PathStyle = "NERETH"`: marble and limestone treads and pavers, no vault lanterns. Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/SunkenSanctuary.luau`, 15 spec-checked footprints): the Bell Stack's open shrine of lancet bays under a golden scallop canopy (its crest about 150 studs over the surf) with the first great Tidebell hung high over the Alpha's camp; bell arches over the First Flight and the Bell Stair with aqua rune tablets; the landing's sheared edge and piers; the fallen flight and the drowned bell in the gap; the Hollin barge (a graft-knot cider barrel, a Pale Band rag); the Tide Claws' shell midden; the tide-queen and tide-king colossi sunk to the knee in the surf; a surf colonnade and broken bell arches standing in the sea; rockpools with anemones and a spill-fall; and the **Tidewall**, the sea standing like a cliff along the north wall with fish shoals in it and the drowned city's spires beyond, plus coral, sea fans, kelp, clams and seagrass. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_sunken_f36_kit.py` (17 templates, each with a part-built fallback); grade: bright turquoise noon, the clearest Sunken floor (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[36]`).

Across the wide north garth (`>= 42` studs of impassable open water, kept open by `Sheer` aprons on *The Lavabo*, *Chapter Isle*, *Cantors' Walk* and *Coral Walk*) rises *Chapter Isle* (Apex, Y 40), reached only by circling round *Cantors' Walk* (Y 20) or *Coral Walk* (Y 24) and climbing one of the twin coral-arched *Isle Stairs* (`STAIRS`, `BridgeStyles = "ARCADE"`, columns outside `UsableWidth` and arches >= 20 studs clear). *Bell Tower* (Y 36) joins the two walks behind the isle without linking to it; side spurs house *The Dorter* (Y 4), *The Frater* (deck, Y 10), *Choir Loft* (deck, Y 26), *Sacristy* (Y 28) and *Scriptorium* (deck, Y 30).   - **Floor 38, Tidal Channels (`sunken:3`, `TERRAIN_VARIATION`):** the Nereth waterworks under a brooding grey-teal storm tide (`700 × 740 × 265`, `Bottom = -6`, `Surface = "DEEPWARD"`, `PathStyle = "DEEPWARD"`, `WallBand = { Base = 26, Noise = 9, NorthScale = 0.22 }`; guide image `reference/Sunken Sanctuary 38.jpg`, plan `docs/prompts/sunken-sanctuary-floor-layouts-prompt.md`). 16 platforms, 19 links and 15 walk heights (strictly beating every earlier third floor: 3, 8, 13, 18, 23, 28 and 33; 25 live camp mobs, 3 Elites, 2 tailwater Basin return ramps, and `ReplacePatrols` with `TAIL_GATE`, `WEST_FLIGHT`, `EAST_FLIGHT` and `HEAD_RUNG` alongside `BASIN_LOOP`). **Shape: a twin-rail ladder climbing five stepped canal locks.** *Lock-keeper's Stair* descends to *Tailwater Quay* (Y 0), then west (*West Towpath* Y 12 → *West Lockhouse* Y 20 → *Upper West Lock* Y 28) and east (*East Towpath* Y 16 → *East Lockhouse* Y 24 → *Upper East Lock* Y 32) towpath rails climb alongside five stepped `Terrain.Locks` chambers (`WaterLevel` stepping `-2 → 4 → 10 → 16 → 22`), bridged across the canal at *Tail Gate*, *Middle Gate* and *Head Gate* (`BridgeStyles = "LOCKGATE"` timber-and-bronze lock bridges with hanging mitre gates, overflow ribbons and overhead bell gallows). Flanking side channels hold the *Tide Mill*, *Sluice House*, *Eel Weir*, *Upper Pound* and *Tidebell Tower* (Y 44), while twin stairs (*HeadGate_Apex* and *UpperWest_Apex*) ascend to *The Sea-Gate* Apex (back-west, Y 48). Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/SunkenFloor38.luau`, 23 spec-checked footprints): the **168.5-stud** open *Sea-Gate* framed by colossal trident statues looking straight into the *Storm Tidewall* and distant viaduct aqueduct, the **149.2-stud** slender gothic *Tidebell Tower*, five lock-chamber mitre gates with aqua tide-wave runes and overflow curtains, four lockhouse bell gallows, jammed Lock 2 and Upper Pound barges, *Tide Mill* with foaming undershot waterwheel, *Sluice House* with triple foaming discharge arches, curved timber *Eel Weirs*, sluice winches, tide-gauge columns, harbour bollards and tailwater wrecks. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_sunken_f38_kit.py` (13 templates in `TowerBiomeKit.ExtraTemplates`); grade: overcast grey-teal storm haze with glowing aqua rune bloom (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[38]`).
  - **Floor 39, Pearlguard Vault (`sunken:4`, `ELITE_STRONGHOLD`):** the Pearlguard's double-walled sea fortress and harbour vault protecting the great Pearl Vault (`780 × 795 × 290`, `Bottom = -6`, `Surface = "DEEPWARD"`, `PathStyle = "DEEPWARD"`, `WallBand = { Base = 24, Noise = 8, NorthScale = 0.20 }`, `NoApexShelf = true`; guide image `reference/Sunken Sanctuary 39.jpg`). 17 terraces, 20 links and 16 walk heights (strictly beating Floor 38 and Floors 4, 9, 14, 19, 24, 29 and 34; 35 live camp mobs, 4 Elites with `UPPER_WEST` Y 36 and `UPPER_EAST` Y 40 above Y 30, 2 harbour return ramps, and `ReplacePatrols` with `OUTER_WARD`, `WALL_WALK`, `WEST_CLIMB`, `EAST_CLIMB` and `VAULT_RING`). **Shape: two concentric sea-walls with a vault behind them.** From *Breakwater Steps* (Sanctuary) and the *Harbour Apron* (Basin, Y 0), **only** the portcullis *Sea Gate* (`Crossing`, Y 8) enters the fortress (outer-gate rule), flanked by the sunken harbour *Divers' Wharf* (wood deck, Y 10) and *Oyster Pearl Beds* (deck, Y 18). Beyond the outer curtain (*Pearl Market* Y 12, *Muster Yard* Y 16, *Harbour Ward* Y 20, *Cistern* Y 24) stands the unbroken Inner Sea-Wall anchored by *Coral Gate Tower* (`WestBastion`, Y 36) and *Tide Tower* (`EastBastion`, Y 40) joined across the *Drowned Gate* by the elevated `BridgeStyles = "WALLWALK"` *Wall-Walk* bridge (inner-wall rule: removing both towers disconnects the vault). From arrival the inner wall hides the lower inner ward while only the gleaming 218-stud **Pearl Vault** dome looms over its battlements; past the towers, twin inner climbs (*Warden's Hall* Y 44 → *Shell Chapel* Y 50, or *Reliquary* Y 46 → *Vault Stair* deck Y 48 → `STAIRS` +8) converge on the back-east *Pearl Vault* (`Apex`, `160, 56, −280`). Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/SunkenFloor39.luau`, 26 spec-checked footprints): the **218-stud** `PearlVaultDome` greenhouse vault housing the luminous `ColossalClam` with pearl orb behind the Apex; `PortcullisGatehouse` over the Sea Gate; `DrownedGateWall` with sea-serpent reliefs and seven battered `SeaWallSegment` curtains; `CoralGateTower` and `TideArmillaryTower`; `DiversWharfBell`, `ShellAwningStall` market stalls, `MuffledBellStack` barricades, `OysterRack` stakes, `HarbourCrane`, two `PearlguardGalley` ships and `HarbourChainBoom` in the harbour; `CoralWardenStatue` guardians, `CoralCageReliquary`, and the towering north `TidewallFortressNorth` wall of standing sea holding the vast dark shadow of the Leviathan. Kit: `tools/blender/generate_sunken_f39_kit.py` (15 templates); grade: deep sea-blue twilight with golden-pearl vault glow (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[39]`).
  - **Floor 40, Leviathan's Basin (`sunken:5`, `BOSS_ARENA`, Biome Boss: Tidal Leviathan):** the inner basin of the drowned Great Temple beneath the closed **Sea-Dome** (`860 × 795 × 330`, `Bottom = -10`, `Surface = "DEEPWARD"`, `PathStyle = "DEEPWARD"`, `Terrain.Maelstrom` spiral-ribbed whirlpool basin, `NoApexShelf = true`, `WallBand = { Base = 22, Noise = 8, NorthScale = 0.20 }`; guide image `reference/Sunken Sanctuary 40.jpg`). 19 terraces (18 combat/traversal + Sanctuary), 23 links and 18 distinct walk heights (strictly beating Floor 39 and every earlier fifth floor: 5, 10, 15, 20, 25, 30 and 35; 35 live camp mobs, 4 Elites, the **Tidal Leviathan** Boss camp on the `196 × 144` *Leviathan's Dais* at Y 64, 2 maelstrom return ramps at `X ≈ ±318.2`, and `ReplacePatrols` with `WEST_HORN`, `EAST_HORN`, `TIDE_GATE`, `WEST_CREST`, `EAST_CREST` and `CHAIN_RING` alongside `BASIN_LOOP`). **Shape: two bioluminescent reef horns climb round a living whirlpool maelstrom to twin suspended chain-bridge stairs onto a sheer central pillar dais in its eye.** From *The Last Bell* (Sanctuary) and *Wrack Shore* (Basin, Y 0), both *West Horn* (*West Shoal* Y 8 → *West Reef* Y 20 → *West Chain Tower* Y 36 → *West Anchor* Y 52 → `STAIRS` +12) and *East Horn* (*East Shoal* Y 10 → *East Reef* Y 22 → *East Chain Tower* Y 38 → *East Anchor* Y 56 → `STAIRS` +8) wrap around the basin (with optional higher crest loops through *West/East Crest* and *High Cantor's Pulpit* / *Pearl Reliquary*, plus *Kelp Shrine* and *Bell Barge* spurs) and enter *Leviathan's Dais* via **exact two** `BridgeStyles = "CHAIN"` suspended chain-bridge stairs; *Tide Gate* (`Crossing`, Y 12) links both reefs via `"CAUSEWAY"` spans and juts out to the dead-end *Drowned Altar* (Y 26) facing the dais across the vortex. Set pieces (`TowerSetPieces/SunkenFloor40.luau`, 26 spec-checked footprints): the **272-stud** torn twin-buttress `GreatTidebellTower` stump north of the dais with its twisted bronze yoke and shattered bell remnant (never overhanging the arena or portals); overhead geodesic `SeaDomeShell` and glowing `EyeOfDaylight` oculus with client-animated orbiting Leviathan/whale/manta/shark silhouettes (`SunkenDomeSwimmer`); **client-animated `MaelstromVortex`** (flush animated water swirls, subtle water-tornado funnels, swirling debris, and quality-scaled eye spray & bubble columns driven client-side by `MaelstromVortexController.client.luau` at zero server cost); clean flat dais arena with flush blue-and-gold trident mosaic, outer-rim `DaisBrazierPedestal`s, hanging pillar banners, and four rim waterfalls; twin `BrokenTideStatue` monarchs, `ChainAnchorBollard`s (plus snapped trailing chain), `ChainTowerRound`s, `DrownedAltar`, `CantorPulpit`, `AbyssReliquaryShrine`, `BellBargeWreck`, `KelpShrine`, drowned perimeter colonnade, and violet/lavender/pale-ochre/cyan-aqua table-coral reefs (zero red or orange). Kit: `tools/blender/generate_sunken_f40_kit.py` (15 templates); grade: storm-eye under the sea (`HubLighting.FloorGrades[40]`).
  - **Astral Archives (Floors 41–45, the Halcyr):** the **Halcyr** were star-archivists who believed every star was a book and built an open-sky celestial library in vellum-white stone, lapis-indigo marble, brass, gold leaf and ebony. Their **Index Engine** (Floor 43) catalogued every star except one blank entry — the **Rift**, a tear leading down toward the Abyssal Throne (Floors 46–50) watched by the **Rift Warden** (Floor 45). When the last Grand Indexer ordered the Engine to write the unwritten entry, the Rift read the Archive back: the Index fractured, the Runeguard sealed the Athenaeum (Floor 44), and pages, lamps and stars now drift toward the rift-eye. Players follow the **Index Lamps** (star-quill lanterns in brass rings showing open routes) while the client-only **Conjunction** (`StarterPlayerScripts/Rendering/Conjunction.luau`, synced to `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`, zero remotes, zero gameplay effect) sweeps starlight across tagged star-glyphs, brightens lamps, lifts loose pages, nudges bloom and rings a far chime (plus a low Rift undertone on 43–45), tightening from 16 s on Floor 41 to 6 s on Floor 45. All five rooms are open-sky Starfields using `PathStyle = "HALCYR"` and the `HALCYR` (41–42) / `RIFTWARD` (43–45) terrain surfaces.

  - **Abyssal Throne (Floors 46–50, the Vahl):** the **Vahl** were mortal wardens who followed **Vharos the Warden-King** beneath the Rift to bind the Abyss from within in black obsidian, blackened iron chains, bone-white marble walkway trim and matte wine-crimson silk under the **Chained Crown** sigil. Below every walkway the **Undertow** braids and climbs uphill toward the Throne, while pale bone-white **Wardlights** in iron cages mark every open way (all gameplay telegraphs keep walk surfaces free of crimson/orange/violet emissive glare per §5.7). The client-only **Dirge** (`StarterPlayerScripts/Rendering/Dirge.luau`, synced to `workspace:GetServerTimeNow()`, zero remotes, zero gameplay effect) tolls a slow far bell, surges the Undertow, flares Wardlights, draws chains taut, lifts ash/page drift, nudges bloom (`+0.05..0.08`) and bows distant court silhouettes, tightening from 16 s on Floor 46 to 6 s on Floor 50 (where the Crimson Eclipse and Throne's heart-crack answer with a deep red-black heartbeat). All five rooms use open-vault black skyboxes with `PathStyle = "VAHL"` and the `VAHL` (46–47) / `THRONEWARD` (48–50) terrain surfaces.

- **Authored room size (`TowerLayout` `Size`, Floor 21 first):** an authored floor may override its floor-in-biome footprint with `Size = { Width, Depth, Height, Bottom? }`; every consumer reads the built layout's `Width`, `Depth`, `Height` and `Bottom`, and the 2,400-stud room grid still holds the largest planned room (920 deep). Every other floor keeps the per-index table above.
- **Authored terrain (`TowerLayout` `Terrain`, Floor 6 first):** an authored floor can shape its own ground as data. `Direct = true` makes natural terraces, landings and ramps the terrain itself: their parts stay as invisible, non-colliding proxies, and the field sits 2 studs under the walking plane (smooth voxel terrain renders its surface half a voxel above the requested height, exactly so only on 4-stud planes, so terrain terraces stand on multiples of 4), 2.8 under stair treads and 4.5 under built `Decks` (solid, visible masonry; `DeckThickness` makes a deck a thin bridge that raises no ground, so its pool runs on beneath it). Where walkways overlap the highest surface wins, and a ramp wins a tie with a deck so it meets the deck flush. Aprons fall away along noise-warped edges, every path gets a trodden `TRAIL` spine, and a `Surface` palette (`TowerTerrain.Surfaces`) says what trails, walk ground, banks, cliffs and pool beds are made of. `Bridges` hang a path as a solid plank walkway with the chasm open beneath it, `RoundDecks` make a deck a disc (a cap top), `Shapes` round a terrain terrace into a squircle (its corners fall away, its edges' middles stay flat for the paths), `Pools` (ellipses) replace the random chasm pools, `Islands` rise out of them, `LiquidRise` and `FloorDip` set the water level and pool depth, `WallBand` the width of the mountain band, `NoApexShelf` drops the ridge that otherwise rises behind the Apex (for a dais that stands free), and `GroveScale`, `GroundCover`/`GroundCoverCount` and `CliffVeins` tune the ecosystem. Outside `Terrain`, `Portals = {Side, Spread}` moves the boss portals to the Apex's south rim. Because terraces stop answering raycasts, the arrival check, loot drops and mob grounding also raycast the Terrain (ignoring water). Terrain colours are place-wide, so each colour belongs to one look: Cobblestone, Concrete, Pavement and Asphalt are Fungal-owned (violet cobble, spore-moss, indigo slate, wet teal-black pool beds) and no biome palette may use them.
- **Natural floors (`TowerTerrain.luau`, once the Tower Biome Kit is imported):** every floor stands in its own voxel landscape. The chasm is rolling ground with marsh pools, lava crusts, frozen ponds or crystal flats by biome; noisy, partly terraced mountain walls rise past the ceiling on every side, so the room's box walls disappear; every terrace becomes a plateau on a rugged rock apron; every ramp runs over an earthen land bridge; and the shelf behind the Apex rises to it so the backdrop monuments stand on ground. Terrain materials come from the biome (`TowerTerrain.Palettes`: grass and leafy grass over rock in the Ruins, snow and glacier in the Citadel, basalt and cracked lava in the Foundry...). On standard natural floors the terrain stays a full voxel (`4.5` studs) under part-built walkways and falls away at `1.4` studs per stud or steeper; floors using live `Terrain` directly as grassy ground (`verdant:1`, `verdant:2`, and grounded banks/entry meadows/return ramps on `verdant:3`, `verdant:4`, and `verdant:5`) align the smooth voxel surface at `ownSurface - RES/2` (`-2.0` studs) with packed `Ground` trails and 3D `Grass`, keep grassy `Part` colliders disabled so visual terrain matches physical footing, and reserve solid `Part`s for built masonry (`IsDeck` viaducts, fortress/amphitheatre decks, suspended bridges, and `IsStair` stairs — keeping suspended platforms/links out of terrain embankment buildup on `verdant:3`, `verdant:4`, and `verdant:5` so rivers, moats, and cenotes flow cleanly underneath). A mountain may only rise beside a walkway as a canyon wall, past a 6-stud moat and well above it; terraces lose their mosaics, masonry bands and corner pieces, ramps lose their inlays, lanterns and viaduct piers (their curbs stay as invisible railings), and the chasm's Ground slab stays as an invisible catch. A retiring room clears its terrain. Over the terrain the kit is scattered as an ecosystem (`TowerBiomeKit.Ecology`, TowerBiomeAssets section 7), seeded per floor and scaled to the floor's size: groves on open ground and on the big terraces' lawns (a centrepiece tree, smaller companions and an understory ring), a loose meadow of understory, understory and boulders along the plateau rims, trees climbing the mountain slopes, boulders, rock outcrops half sunk in the slopes and a few monoliths, spaced apart and kept out of every camp and walkway (a tree keeps only its trunk off a path; its canopy may overhang it high above heads). These props are placed on the terrain's own surface (`OnTerrain`), so the settle pass leaves them alone. Terraces take the biome's ground material (grass, snow, sand...). Only the tower floors use voxel terrain, so `TowerTerrain.MaterialColors` sets lusher grass, leafy grass, mud, ground and rock colours place-wide. Biomes grow their own: banyans over bushes and ruins in the Ruins, mushroom forests in the Hollow, dead trees and boulder fields in the Dunes, pine forests in the Citadel and Highlands, forge stacks in the Foundry, blighted trees and pods in the Grove, giant coral in the Sanctuary, crystal groves in the Archives and obsidian shard fields in the Abyss. The part-made perimeter cliffs, chasm islands, seas, basin pools and clutter beds are not built on natural floors; the ramp arches (now square to each ramp), the iconic guardians and the Apex backdrops are.
- **Multi-Tiered Spatial Topography (`TowerLayout.luau`):**
  Each room features 5 navigable elevation zones:
  1. **Elevated Safe Overlook ($Y = +8$):** South landing sanctuary housing arrival and extraction fixtures.
  2. **Foreground Basin ($Y = 0\text{ to }+2$):** Open entry courtyard and elemental basin connected by ceremonial ramps.
  3. **Flanking Galleries & Central Crossing ($Y = +4\text{ to }+12$):** Elevated West/East terraces and split bridges over recessed elemental channels.
  4. **Upper Bastions ($Y = +10\text{ to }+16$):** Fortified wings and guardian platforms.
  5. **Apex Sanctum / Boss Dais ($Y = +16\text{ to }+28$):** Grand tiered staircase ascending to the biome apex or Boss arena.
  *Slopes are restricted to $\le 15^\circ$ and stair rises to $\le 1.0\text{ stud}$ per tread. Links have stable connector IDs, a usable corridor, and full-width flat landings with eight studs of extra approach width. The inclined deck ends outside the upper terrace's entire shoulder footprint; its flat landing bridges back into the terrace. Short links extend at their lower end to preserve the slope limit. Curbs stop two studs before the deck ends, and crossing terrace trim is cleared from landing footprints. Basin return ramps start at the actual chasm floor (`Bottom - 4`) and have matching landings. Geometry and clearance share the `TowerLayout` connector contract; see [Tower Navigation Architecture](TOWER_NAVIGATION.md).*
- **Floor Safe Sanctuary & Relic Banking (`CanBankCargo = true`):**
  The elevated south arrival sanctuary ($128 \times 56\text{ studs}$ at $Y = +8$) is designated with `CombatSafe = true`, `CanBankCargo = true`, and `CanRecoverWipe = true`:
  - **Automatic banking (no pad or altar):** entering the sanctuary while carrying a relic (`BankArmed = true`) immediately banks it into permanent profile currency with 100% relic value and the active Market multiplier (`EXTRACT_SAFE_ZONE`).
  - **Travel portals:** 4 ProximityPrompt portals (`PREVIOUS`, `NEXT`, `SELECTOR`, `HUB`) along the rear of the sanctuary (Phase H).
- **Procedural Landmark Kits (`TowerBiomeAssets.luau`):**
  Procedural landmark kits for all 10 biomes (statues, bridges, falls, crystals, flora, overhead cogs, celestial orreries, chained obelisks) with local lights.
  - **Tower Biome Kit (Blender meshes):** the props are drawn with 30 sculpted templates modelled in Blender by `tools/blender/generate_tower_biome_kit.py` (`assets-source/tower/biome_kit/TowerBiomeKit.fbx`, about 43k triangles in 79 meshes; contact sheets in `previews/`). Rock-column cliffs (two variants) and a flat-topped column island dress the perimeter cliffs, corner massifs, horizon peaks, ledges, foothills, chasm islands and the Apex shelf; a ruined arch is built from a plinth, stacked masonry courses (tiled, so the tall ramp-arch piers keep their proportions) and a keystoned crown scaled to the span; carved obelisks (furnace stacks in the Foundry, obsidian shard clusters in the Abyss) and crystal clusters replace the spires; a banyan-rooted ruin tree, a blighted tree with glowing fissures, a dead tree, a tiered pine, a giant glowing mushroom, a lion guardian and two robed statues (staff and trident) replace the part-built ones; and each biome has its own clutter (bushes, rubble and urns, mushroom clusters, boulders, ice crystals, forge scrap, cairns, blight pods, coral, tome stacks, void shards). Frozen Citadel landmarks add a snow-laden pine and Thunder Highlands' grow highland pines. Every template is split into colour roles (`<Template>__<ROLE>`: stone, trim, accent, moss/snow/ash cover, glow, wood, foliage, metal, gold, crystal, cap, stem, coral, alt), so one mesh serves every biome: `TowerKitProps` tints each role from the floor theme and the biome palette in `Shared/Config/TowerBiomeKit.luau` (moss in the Ruins, snow in the Citadel, cracked lava in the Foundry, cosmic dust in the Archives). Placed meshes never collide or take queries; solid pieces (cliffs, ledges, islands, arch piers, spire shafts) keep their old part as an invisible collider and support, so navigation, settling and clearance are unchanged, and each mesh model carries `PropKind`, so clearance still removes a prop whole. The templates live in `ServerStorage.TowerBiomeKit` (not managed by Rojo): import the FBX with File > Import 3D, run `tests/studio-tower-biome-kit-import.luau` (it measures the importer's scale and axes from the shared Blender origin and files each template with its origin on the ground, front +Z), and save the place. Floors use the core kit only when every template is present (`TowerKitProps.Ready`); until then they build the part-made props. Optional per-floor sculpted sets (such as Floor 2's 5 templates from `tools/blender/generate_verdant_f2_kit.py` -> `assets-source/tower/verdant_f2_kit/VerdantF2Kit.fbx`) register in `TowerBiomeKit.ExtraTemplates` and check `TowerKitProps.Has(name)` independently so floors render crisp native fallbacks if the extra FBX has not yet been imported.
  - **Grounded clutter rule:** no prop floats. Every prop stands on a registered support (a terrace, island crown, cliff tier, foothill shelf or the rock shelf behind the Apex) or on the chasm floor (the top of the Ground slab, `layout.Bottom - 4`). Companion pieces, arch piers and tree roots that would overhang a support are dropped, run down to real ground, or pulled in. Every compound prop (a tree, spire, mushroom, arch, statue, clutter cluster, terrace outcrop or brazier, ramp curb) is a Model tagged `PropKind`, and raised supports (chasm islands, perimeter cliffs, horizon foothills) own the props standing on them. Camp and lane clearance (`TowerPropClearance`) removes a prop or support whole when any of its parts is in the way, so no canopy, cap or spire tip is ever left floating. A rock shelf level with the Apex fills the space behind it for the backdrop to stand on. After a floor is built, a settle pass lowers any prop still hovering onto the support under it (props with nothing below are removed; rings, chains, gears and sky pieces hang by design), then camp and lane clearance run again. `tests/studio-tower-prop-audit.luau` builds all 50 floors in Studio and reports orphaned and floating props and props on walkways, which must all be 0.
  - **Clear walkway rule:** `TowerLayout.Lanes` lists the walkways: every ramp and stair with a 3-stud shoulder, the authored full-width landing footprints at both ends, the 40-wide Basin causeway and the 36-wide Sanctuary exit (arrival to front edge). No scenery stands on them. Any prop with a part on a walkway is removed whole, solid or not; the ramps, terraces, curbs, causeway inlay, the arches that span the ramps and faint light shafts are exempt. Chasm islands step outward (or along the room) from their authored spot until they clear every walkway, and their waterfalls face away from the central ramps.
  - **Shared movement & destination policies (`HeroNavigation.luau`, `TowerNavigationGraph.luau`, `SquadMovement.luau`):** heroes and enemies use feet-position navigation with cached connector topology, height-aware ramp membership, supported body sweeps, and bounded asynchronous path requests (`MAX_IN_FLIGHT = 6`). Moving goals preserve connector progress. Follow and pursuit share signed-progress recovery: route refresh after $1\text{ s}$ without progress, unreachable after $3\text{ s}$; attacks pause the timer. Active floor runs sample a bounded server trail of supported player movement (`MAX_TRAIL_POINTS = 48`) with forward lookahead, connector-graph fallback across jumped gaps, and width-aware formation slots that compress on ramps and expand on platforms. Assigned squads compute a stable engagement/regroup anchor once per squad tick using connector route cost (`SquadMovement.RouteCost`), ignore stranded-hero drag, retry alternative supported attack positions within range before applying a $5\text{ s}$ generation-scoped target exclusion, and operate independently of the owner's sprint state or physical location. Ordinary follow lag never triggers a distance/stuck teleport; explicit Recall (`E`) remains. See [Tower Navigation Architecture](TOWER_NAVIGATION.md).
  - **Sanctuary and portals (Phase H):** each floor's Sanctuary (128 × 56 studs; there is no altar or pad: relics bank automatically anywhere inside it) is traced by a glowing square on the exact `ARRIVAL` zone with a gap and two pylons at the front exit, a crystal beacon with a faint light column at each corner (visible from the Apex), and a "SANCTUARY" label over the exit. The four travel stations are **portals** (Scale 1.2, 24 studs apart; the boss portals are Scale 1.3) (Previous, Next, Choose Floor, Return to Hub) built by `TowerPortalKit` from one Blender model (`assets-source/tower/portal/TowerPortal.blend`/`.fbx`; four meshes: stone frame, gold trim, runes, vortex; about 9.8k triangles). Each biome tints the stone and gold; the vortex colour tells the actions apart (Next in the biome light, Previous dimmer, Choose Floor gold, Hub white), and Previous on floor 1 and Next on floor 50 stand dark and disabled. They keep their `TowerAction` prompts (F). Until the meshes' MeshIds are filled in (`Shared/Config/TowerPortalAssets.luau`), the kit builds the same design from parts. **When a floor's boss falls, two portals rise at the back of the Apex** (`TowerLayout.PortalSpots`, kept clear like walkways): **Ascend** to the next floor (Return to Hub on floor 50) and **Rerun**, which sends the Master and squad back to this floor's Sanctuary with a fresh kill counter (and clears their leftover surge pack) so the boss can be summoned again. Both are hold-E prompts that need the Master to have cleared this floor, and everyone on the floor gets a toast when they open.
  - **Chasm groves:** up to 28 tall, rooted props per floor rise from the chasm floor around the raised terraces and beside the ramps, staying clear of every ramp and the Basin return ramps. Each biome uses its own set: canopy trees and ivy-wrapped ruin columns (Verdant), giant mushrooms (Fungal), sandstone hoodoos and dead trees (Ashen; no trees in lava), frosted pines and ice spires (Frozen), furnace stacks and basalt hoodoos (Infernal), pines and rock hoodoos (Thunder), blighted trees and mushrooms (Corrupted), coral towers and marble columns (Sunken), crystal spires and marble columns (Astral), and obsidian spires and hoodoos (Abyssal). Small clutter frames the two ends of each ramp, on the terrace itself.
- **Multi-Area Spatial Mob Camps & AI Leashing (`FirstBiomeCombat.luau`, `TowerEncounterRuntime.luau`):** Camps respawn as a wave 10 s after their last member falls and scale up with every extra Master on the floor (Section 2.5).
  - **Camp roster rule (`CampRoster.luau`):** each live camp (spawning area) fields **all** of the biome's Regular types: members are dealt types from a shuffled bag, so every camp holds each type, in a random mix. Members stand at random spots within the camp's radius (up to 20 studs, at least 8 apart), not on fixed offsets. The floor keeps a fixed number of Elites (1 / 2 / 3 / 4 / 4 on floors 1–5 of a biome, never in the Apex Sanctum, and from floor 3 at least one above the Basin), but which camps they lead is random. The moment a camp's last member dies its next wave is planned: a fresh mix of Regular types, new spots, and its Elite, if it has one, moves to another cleared camp without an Elite (it stays put when none is cleared). A camp that is fighting never changes, and a lone survivor's partial respawn keeps the camp's plan. Rolls are seeded per floor, room generation, camp and wave. Kill points are unchanged: Regulars stay 1, Elites stay 3, and the Elite count per floor never changes.
  - **Patrol rule (`PatrolPath.luau`, Phase E):** besides camps, patrols walk the lanes so a floor never feels static. Every floor has the **Basin loop** (Basin west, foot of the Crossing ramp, Basin east, along the back half of the Basin away from the arrival ramp); from the third floor of a biome one more route is picked at random: a **Gallery** route (Gallery centre, along its link to the Apex on floor 3 or to its Bastion on floors 4–5) or, on floors 4–5, the **Bastion ring** (West Bastion, across the Apex edge, East Bastion). Routes never use the Sanctuary link. A patrol is three mixed Regulars (dealt like camp members) in a fixed formation; from biome 4 it brings the floor's support half the time. Its anchor walks the route back and forth at 5 studs/s while every member keeps formation; when any member engages, the whole patrol stops and fights, leashed 45 studs from where it engaged, then walks back into formation and carries on. Members show the `PATROL` state while walking. A wiped patrol returns as a group 20 s later at its route start, but waits while a Master or hero stands within 40 studs of that start. Patrols count toward the 48 live-mob budget and are the first thing skipped when it is full; their kills count like any Regular's.
  - **Affixed Elites (`AffixRules.luau`, Phase F):** affixes are a **chance summon**. Each time a camp Elite spawns it may emerge **affixed**: 10% in biome 1, rising 1.5% a biome to 23.5% in biome 10. An affixed Elite carries one affix (two from biome 7, never Shielded with Warded), shows them in its name plate ("Frenzied, Hasted Glacier Knight") over a crimson-gold ring, and is announced to everyone on the floor. It **drops relics 4× as often** (its tier roll stays the Elite table, so Abyssal Cores stay Elite-only) and pays 1.5× Gold; kill points are unchanged. Floor Alphas and Biome Bosses never roll affixes.
  - **Timed tower events (`TowerEventService`, `TowerEventConfig.luau`, Phase G):** every server runs one cycle: 15 minutes calm, a 60-second warning (toast to everyone), then a 5-minute event, picked by weight and never the same twice in a row. Biome events feature one biome the server has unlocked (up to the biome holding the floor after the best clear among its players). Difficulty events never touch floors 1–5. State is published as `ReplicatedStorage` attributes (`TowerEventPhase`, `TowerEventId`, `TowerEventStartsAt`, `TowerEventEndsAt`, `TowerEventBiome`), so late joiners need no remote. **Modifiers are stamped on each mob when it spawns, and camps reset at both ends of an event:** when an event starts and again when it ends, every camp and patrol on the floors it touches despawns its current mobs (including surge and split packs) and respawns fresh from the floor layout on the next sync, with respawn timers cleared and the camp roster re-rolled. The Apex boss is left alone. So a whole floor picks up an event's modifiers the moment it starts and drops them the moment it ends (`TowerEventRules.AffectedFloors`, `TowerEncounterRuntime:ResetCamps`). In Studio, `workspace.TowerEventForce = "<EventId>"` starts that event's warning at once. **Gathering players (plan B4):** **Relic Rain** comes round at least once every 3 events (`RelicDroughtCycles`: after 3 others in a row it is picked next, never twice running); its relics have no owner, but whoever carries one can be challenged to a Relic Duel. **Event starts are pushed clear of raids**: an event's warning and run never overlap a raid's warning, fight and results, with 30 s between them (`TowerEventRules.ClearOfRaids`, `RaidGapSeconds`). During the warning and the event the HUD badge has a **GO** button (above the badge on phones) that travels to the event (`TowerRemotes.EventTravel`): a biome event's first floor in the featured biome, a tower-wide event's floor the Master's highest reached, through the normal travel checks; a Master who hasn't reached it, or is already on an affected floor, gets a toast instead. *A Hub board for the countdown was not built; the HUD badge already counts down to the next event.*

    | Event | Scope | Monsters | Rewards |
    | --- | --- | --- | --- |
    | Calamity Hour | Whole tower, floor 6+ | +60% HP, +35% ATK, every Elite emerges affixed | Relic drop chance x2; 30% of Geodes become Urns, 15% of an Elite's Urns become Cores |
    | Elite Tide | Featured biome, floor 6+ | Every live camp is Elite-led | Abyssal Core share x1.5 |
    | Relic Rain | Featured biome | — | Every 30 s a telegraphed meteor (3 s purple disc) lands an unowned relic on a ramp of each featured floor in play; contested loot |
    | Swarm | Whole tower, floor 6+ | Camps respawn in 4 s | Kill progress x1.5 |
    | Golden Tide | Whole tower | — | Gold x2 (Market still applies) |
    | Alpha Hunt | Featured biome | — | Calamity Heart share x3 on Alphas and Biome Bosses |

    Relic modifiers never move Hearts off Alphas and Bosses or Cores off Elites. **HUD:** an event badge sits bottom right above Squad Power (icon, name, timer, gilded bar; calm shows an hourglass and "Next event", the warning pulses gold, an active event takes its accent colour), with a hover/tap tooltip (description, scope and effect lines), and a centre pop-up when an event starts that never blocks input and waits while a full-screen panel is open. Icons live in `GrimoireIcons` (`Event*`); see `docs/ui/tower-event-hud-mockup.html`.

    | Affix | Effect |
    | --- | --- |
    | Shielded | Starts with a shield of 30% MaxHP that returns after 6 s without being hit |
    | Hasted | +35% move speed, attack cooldowns 25% shorter |
    | Vampiric | Heals 35% of the damage it deals |
    | Frenzied | Below 50% HP: +40% ATK and cooldowns 20% shorter |
    | Splitting | On death, two Regulars of its biome step out; they never respawn and give no kill progress |
    | Warded | Allies within 18 studs take 25% less damage |
    | Commanding | Allies within 20 studs deal +20% damage |
  Enemies spawn in 4 to 7 live spatial camps per floor (16–35 mobs: two Basin flank camps, the Central Crossing from floor 2, both Galleries, and both Bastions from floor 4; the Basin centre below the arrival ramp stays clear), plus the Apex Sanctum where the floor's Alpha or Biome Boss is summoned with ground-raycasted elevations and localized leash radiuses ($60\text{ studs}$ for regular/elite packs, $95\text{ studs}$ for Boss encounters), allowing tactical, room-by-room progression without premature floor aggro pulls.
- **Room Lifecycle:** Rooms transition through `ABSENT -> BUILDING -> READY -> RETIRING -> ABSENT` with a 60-second retirement grace period when unoccupied.

#### Biome Catalog (50 Floors across 10 Biomes):
1. **Verdant Ruins (Floors 1–5):** Overgrown Threshold, Broken Aqueduct, Rootway Crossing, Vinebound Bastion, **Mossbound Court (Biome Boss: Mossbound Guardian)**.
2. **Fungal Hollow (Floors 6–10):** Sporelit Entry, Mycelium Galleries, Blooming Chasm, Rotcap Keep, **Matriarch's Hollow (Biome Boss: Spore Matriarch)**.
3. **Ashen Dunes (Floors 11–15):** Cinder Gate, Buried Caravan, Glasswind Ravine, Sunscar Fortress, **Colossus Dais (Biome Boss: Sandstone Colossus)**.
4. **Frozen Citadel (Floors 16–20):** Rimefall Approach, Frozen Barracks, Shattered Iceway, Whiteguard Keep, **Frostbound Throne (Biome Boss: Frostbound King)**.
5. **Infernal Foundry (Floors 21–25):** Slagworks Entry, Molten Assembly, Furnace Causeway, Ironflame Bulwark, **Titan's Crucible (Biome Boss: Ember Titan)**.
6. **Thunder Highlands (Floors 26–30):** Stormgrass Rise, Thunderhead Outpost, Skybridge Cliffs, Tempest Redoubt, **Roc's Eyrie (Biome Boss: Storm Roc)**.
7. **Corrupted Grove (Floors 31–35):** Blighted Verge, Twisted Orchard, Briar Labyrinth, Heartrot Stronghold, **Sovereign's Grove (Biome Boss: Blight Sovereign)**.
8. **Sunken Sanctuary (Floors 36–40):** Drowned Steps, Coral Cloister, Tidal Channels, Pearlguard Vault, **Leviathan's Basin (Biome Boss: Tidal Leviathan)**.
9. **Astral Archives (Floors 41–45):** Starlit Vestibule, Orbiting Stacks, Fractured Index, Runeguard Athenaeum, **Rift Warden's Observatory (Biome Boss: Rift Warden)**.
10. **Abyssal Throne (Floors 46–50):** Abyssal Descent, Crimson Procession, Voidspan Crossing, Dreadguard Citadel, **Overlord's Throne (Biome Boss: Crimson Overlord)**.


### 3.4 Private Fortress Plot & Outskirts Architectural Layout

To accommodate active fortress management, squad training, commerce, and the passive Outskirts AFK loop, each of the 10 Floating Fortress Islands sits on the outer edge of the Cloister Commons (Section 3.2, top surface $Y = 12$), faces into the hub, and has a citadel island footprint of **$260\text{ studs (Width)} \times 320\text{ studs (Length)}$**.

The island is divided into two distinct, interconnected sectors:
1. **The Grand Citadel & Inner Courtyard (Front / North - $260 \times 180\text{ studs}$):** Heavily fortified with $28\text{-stud}$ high crenellated stone walls, corner watch bastions, and a private entrance gatehouse facing the perimeter drawbridge. Encloses all **Core Main Buildings** (Summoning Altar, War Banner, The Pit, Blacksmith Forge) as well as the internal support facilities (**The Market** and **The Training Grounds**).
2. **The Southern Outskirts (Rear / South - $260 \times 140\text{ studs}$):** The sole exterior zone outside the fortress walls, accessed through the vaulted rear fortress gatehouse. Spans open, rugged frontier terrain, pine trees, and dirt wilderness trails housing **The Outskirts Guard Post**.

```
                        FRONT (Facing the Cloister Commons)
                                        | (Bridge)
                                  [North Gatehouse]
                       +---------------------------------------+
                       |   Plaza & Cyan Private Extraction Pad  |
                       |              (Plot Spawn)              |
                       | [Summoning Altar]   [Blacksmith Forge] |
                       | (Hero Banners)      (Artifact Banner)  |
                       |                                       |
                       |     [Plaza Fountain + Angel Statue]   |
                       |           (God's Blessing)            |
                       | [War Banner +                         |
                       |  Architect's Table]  [The Pit]        |
                       | (Command Post)  (Synthesis & Promo)   |
                       |                                       |
                       | [Training Grounds]       [The Market] |
                       | (Sparring & Dummies)  (Bazaar Stalls) |
                       +-------------------+-------------------+
                                           | (Rear Fortress Gate)
                       ====================+====================
                       |         THE REAR OUTSKIRTS GROUNDS     |
                       |                                       |
                       |          [Wilderness Border Trail]    |
                       |                                       |
                       |          [Outskirts Guard Post]       |
                       |       (Timber Bastion & Patrol Run)   |
                       |                                       |
                       +---------------------------------------+
                                SOUTH (Southern Frontier)
```

#### Detailed Sector Breakdown:

1. **Sector A: Arrival Ramp, Gatehouse & Extraction Plaza (North Front):**
   - **Drawbridge & Gate:** A 40-stud bridge connects the fortress gate to its landing on the Cloister Commons' outer edge. Features an owner-locked collision barrier preventing unauthorized plot infiltration.
   - **Plaza & Extraction Pad:** Glowing cyan illuminated deposit zone ($24 \times 1 \times 24\text{ studs}$) for banking carried relics, geodes, and urns into permanent Gold and Gems.
   - **Beacon Pillar & Plot Spawn:** Center anchor with the Master's avatar beacon projected skyward.

2. **Sector B: Grand Inner Fortress Courtyard (Core Buildings & Interior Facilities):**
   - **Courtyard Grid:** Buildings stand in a 3 x 2 grid either side of a clear walkway from the plot spawn, past the fountain, to the rear gate. Every entrance faces the fountain. North row (by the spawn): Summoning Altar and Blacksmith Forge. Middle row: the Command Post (War Banner and Architect's Table) and The Pit. South row: Training Grounds and Market, which only appear once built in the Fortress Tree.
   - **Summoning Altar (North-West Courtyard):** Towering obsidian obelisk featuring dual runic summoning circles for the **Hero Banners** (Call of the Rift `100 Gems` single / `900 Gems` 10x; A Star's Arrival `200 Gems` / `1,800 Gems`). Weapon acquisition lives across the plaza at the Open Forge (§7.4).
   - **Plaza Fountain, God's Blessing & War Banner (Center Courtyard):** Central triple-tiered Gothic stone fountain in the courtyard center with water basins and blue glow, crowned, once unlocked, by a **marble angel statue** with raised hands holding a lantern of light. The fountain is the **God's Blessing** facility (Section 4.3, Facility D): an `[F]` prompt opens its panel, and the angel brightens with each level. 
   - **Command Post (West Courtyard):** The heraldic War Banner standard on a carved stone pedestal, with an `[F]` prompt opening the **Squad Deploy UI** (`SquadController.luau`), and beside it the **Architect's Table**, whose prompt opens the **Fortress Tree**.
   - **The Pit Sanctuary (East Courtyard):**
     - **Synthesis Chamber:** Fiery cauldron station with `[F]` prompt opening the **Synthesis UI** (`SynthesisController.luau`) for feeding EXP fodder.
     - **Promotion Altar:** Consecrated marble star dais with `[F]` prompt opening the **Promotion UI** (`PromotionController.luau`) for star ascensions ($1★ \to 7★$).
   - **The Open Forge (North-East Courtyard):** Open-air stone terrace featuring the **Smelter** (`[F]` prompt opening `ForgeWorkbenchController.luau` for Inventory, Equipping, Selling, and Star Ascension $1★ \to 7★$), the **Master Anvil** (click or `[F]` prompt to reroll unlocked pedestals for free), and up to **6 Artifact Pedestals** displaying rolled 3D weapons with overhead Rarity + Name labels and `[F]` buy prompts.
   - **The Market (South-East Inner Wing):** Covered bazaar stalls, coin scales, and ledger counters located inside the fortress commercial quarter. Stationed workers (`Berserker` preferred) boost Infinite Tower kill Gold and banked relic Gold ($+5\%$ to $+40\%+$).
   - **The Training Grounds (South-West Inner Wing):** Cobblestone sparring arena with weapon racks, archery targets, and training dummies located inside the fortress military quarter. Stationed trainers (`Striker` & `Scholar` preferred) boost the Master's overall squad combat power ($+4\%$ to $+32\%+$ Final ATK/HP).

3. **Sector C: The Southern Outskirts (Exterior Wilderness Zone):**
   - **Rear Fortress Archway:** A $24\text{-stud}$ wide vaulted stone passage connecting the inner fortress courtyard directly to the open wilderness behind the citadel.
   - **The Outskirts Guard Post (South Perimeter):** Heavy timber bastion, log palisade barricade, and campfire situated along the winding wilderness trail amidst lush rolling meadow grass, asymmetrical ancient pines, broadleaf oaks, wild berry shrubs, and mossy granite boulders. Stationed Adventurers (`Vanguard` & `Marksman` preferred) venture out along the curved patrol trail to battle ambient wildlife in a lightweight visual loop, auto-generating online Gold every 60 seconds and logging offline accumulation. Other Masters can visit a plot but cannot affect its facilities. Only the Outskirts and its Guard Post lie outside the fortress walls.

### 3.5 The Cloister Commons (Central Hub)

A solid rounded rectangle (480 × 640, corners R 60, top surface $Y = 12$), built by `CloisterBuilder.luau`, in front of the tower gate. There is no outer band or void: the plots bridge straight onto its edge, and the whole hub is one garden. Every player crosses it between their plot and the tower, so it is the hub's social space. It is a Safe Zone: no mobs and no PvP. The scene model is still named `MastersCommons`.

- **Outer edge:** a slate rim wall with crenels and a 20-stud invisible barrier (taller than a jump from the wall top). It has 9 openings: one gate landing per plot (banners in the plot's colour, a nameplate showing the owner, a cyan rune) and the **South Gate**, a pair of 30-stud pylons with an arch reading *The Infinite Tower*, where the Pilgrim's Causeway arrives. Plots 1–3 land on the west edge, 4–5 on the north edge and 6–8 on the east edge.
- **Paths:** a 16-wide cobbled path runs from every plot landing straight to the dais, lit by lamp posts, and one runs north from the dais through the pool. From the South Gate the Pilgrim's Way (braziers, crimson banners, a crimson runner leading on to the dais) runs north to the dais.
- **Centre:** the **Wayfinder Dais**: a compass-rose plaza with 8 cyan arrows pointing to the plots, the Statue of the First Master with sword raised toward the tower, the floating Wayfinder Crystal, and five squad plinths. To the north is the **Reflecting Pool** with stepping stones, a footbridge, lily pads, willows and a fountain jet. East of the pool (its right side seen from the dais), at hub-local `(100, −160)`, stands the **Raid Portal** (§5.6), turned diagonally to face the hub centre.
- **South lawns:** the **Gazebo** (west) and the **Leaderboard Pavilion** (east) either side of the Pilgrim's Way (Top Playtime faces the dais, Top Power faces the South Gate, `SharedLeaderboards.luau`). In the south corners, the **Overlook Terrace** (west), with telescopes aimed at the tower, and the **Biome Stones** (east, 10 menhirs, one per tower biome).
- **West and east lawns:** a cherry-blossom orchard on each side, between the middle and north landings.
- **Garden:** oaks, pines and flower beds are scattered over the remaining lawn, kept clear of the paths, landings and features.

All slopes are $\le 15°$ and steps $\le 1$ stud, except the overlook, which is reached by ramps. Plots fill nearest-to-the-gate first (`HubLayout.AssignmentOrder`). The farthest plots (4 and 5) are about 700 studs' walk from the gate.

### 3.6 Hub Lighting: Sanctuary and Dread

The hub's lighting tells the story: the fortresses are a refuge, the tower is a threat. Presets live in `HubLighting.luau`. The server applies **Sanctuary** as everyone's baseline, and `HubLightingController.client.luau` blends each player's own view by where their character stands. The sun stays fixed (ClockTime 14.8), so blending never swings shadows.

| Preset | Where | Look |
| :--- | :--- | :--- |
| **Sanctuary** | Plots, bridges and the Cloister Commons | Golden afternoon: warm sun, soft blue bounce light, clear air, saturated colour, gentle bloom and sun rays, exposure +0.3 |
| **Dread** | The tower walkway and the tower itself | Crimson murk: dusky red ambient, moderate red haze, slightly drained colour, red glows that bloom, exposure +0.05. Dark in mood but bright enough that the Master, their heroes and the causeway stay readable |
| **Storm** | Off-map tower floors | The moody storm grade, tinted per biome (below) |

On a tower floor, Storm takes its biome's grade (`HubLighting.BiomeGrades`, `HubLighting.TowerPreset`): ambient, colour shift, atmosphere colour and haze, tint, saturation and, for some biomes, exposure, contrast and bloom. Verdant Ruins is golden jungle haze, Fungal Hollow a deep bioluminescent blue, Ashen Dunes hot orange dust, Frozen Citadel cold clear blue, Infernal Foundry forge red, Thunder Highlands violet storm light, Corrupted Grove magenta murk, Sunken Sanctuary aqua, Astral Archives indigo starlight and Abyssal Throne a violet void. A hand-authored floor can layer its own grade over its biome's (`HubLighting.FloorGrades`; floor 1 is dawn). The controller reads the floor from the room grid (`HubLighting.TowerFloorAt`) and blends over about a second when a Master travels to a floor with a different grade; leaving the tower fades out from the last biome's grade.

Dread fades in by distance from the tower centre: none beyond 470 studs (the Commons' south edge at 476 and every plot), full within 395 (the walkway). Crossing the Pilgrim's Causeway is the transition, so the light drains as players approach the tower and returns as they walk home. The presets adjust `ExposureCompensation`, not `Brightness`, so the Settings brightness slider keeps working. The plot bridges use pale warm stone and cream marble rails; crimson is reserved for the tower side.

Performance: sun shadows are **off by default** (Settings → Graphics turns them on; the Cinematic preset does too), scenery lights never cast shadows, and `PrioritizeLightingQuality` is off so lower graphics levels can scale lighting down. Hub torches, braziers, small emitters and beams (fountain waterfalls, causeway arcs) switch off by distance (`HubEffectCulling`), and hub scenery emitters run at half their built rate (`StaticGeometry.ThinEmitters`, original kept in `BaseRate`). There are no volumetric terrain clouds. Glow is kept restrained: one bloom only (the preset's `TowerStormBloom`, intensity 0.25–0.35, threshold 1.2–1.25; Settings → Bloom toggles it), static scenery lights run at 60% of their built brightness (`StaticGeometry`, original kept in `BaseBrightness`), and the Blacksmith Forge chimney has no smoke plume. The running game should look like the place in Edit mode: the preset's `TowerStormColorCorrection` is the only colour grade (the Settings menu adds none, and Colour FX never switches on the HUD's modal-focus effect; with Colour FX off the Atmosphere is thinned to density 0.10 and haze 0, and `HubLightingController` is the only script that writes the Atmosphere, so switching back restores the current zone's values), the default brightness slider (20%) is the place's own 1.65, and the camera-following wind mist stays faint. Lighting uses Roblox's unified lighting (`LightingStyle` Realistic); with `PrioritizeLightingQuality` off, its cost follows each player's graphics level. The place file must have it unticked as well, because its saved value can override the script.

---

## 4. HERO, COMBAT & UI MECHANICS

### 4.1 Gacha Banners & Hero Roster Architecture

Hero summoning at the **Summoning Altar** (`FacilityController.luau`) is partitioned into two distinct banners. Each banner rolls an **inherent rarity tier**, then picks a hero from that tier: most tiers hold one hero, and a tier with a pool (`HeroesPoolByStar`) splits its rate evenly between its heroes. Every hero is summoned at **1★**, whatever its tier, and its rarity sets how strong that 1★ hero is (see *Inherent Hero Rarity* below). Stars are earned only by promotion at The Pit. All hero odds can be raised by the **God's Blessing** facility (Section 4.3, Facility D).

**Summon screen.** Each banner card shows its featured hero (A Star's Arrival: Astrea; Call of the Rift: its Mythical, Han), a line with the top tier's odds and the pity, and a **View Heroes & Odds** button. The button opens a window laid out like the Forge's *All weapons & odds* (`HeroOddsModal.luau`): a rate pill per tier, the banner's pity, 10-pull and price rules, the 50/50 state on A Star's Arrival, the God's Blessing luck, then every tier (rarest first) with each hero it can award, that hero's chance per pull, its 1★ stats, and Featured / Owned markers. All odds come from `HeroBannerOdds`, which mirrors the server roll and applies the player's luck. A tier whose hero has a different rarity says so on the card.

#### 1. Permanent Banner: "Call of the Rift" (Rarity Tiers 1–7)
The permanent standard banner features **at least one character for each rarity from Common to Mythical**, and grows as permanent heroes are added:
- **Summon Cost:** `100 Gems` per single pull (`900 Gems` for 10x pull).
- **Pity System:** Guaranteed **Exalted or higher** hero within **120 summons** (`MaxPity = 120`, `PityMinTier = 5`). The pity pull is split by the banner's Exalted/Legendary/Mythical rates (with God's Blessing luck): about 65% Exalted (Ignis or Diran), 29% Legendary (Edis or Vane), 6% Mythical (Han). Any natural Exalted+ pull resets the counter. This banner has no featured hero and no 50/50 rule.
- **10-Pull Guarantee:** Every 10x pull includes at least one **Rare** or higher hero.
- **First 10-pull (new Masters):** in onboarding arms with the showcase (`v2`, §4.7), a Master's first Call of the Rift 10x, made before any other summon, includes a **Superior** or higher hero instead (`FirstTenPullMinTier = 4`, Aeli unless a rarer hero lands). Superior is below `PityMinTier`, so pity is untouched. While that guarantee is open and the Gems cover a 10x, single pulls and A Star's Arrival pulls are refused with a "Ten-pull first" toast, so the guarantee can't be spent by accident. The odds window states it while it applies (`FirstTenPullShowcase`, a derived profile flag that is never saved).
- **Drop Rates:**
  - `Common (75.14%)`: **Gide** or **Shurn**
  - `Uncommon (18.0%)`: **Aaron**
  - `Rare (4.7%)`: **Catio**
  - `Superior (1.65%)`: **Aeli**
  - `Exalted (0.33%)`: **Ignis** or **Diran**
  - `Legendary (0.15%)`: **Edis Callen** or **Vane** (0.075% each)
  - `Mythical (0.03%)`: **Han** (the same Gems per copy as Astrea, by the same rule)

**Han's rework.** Han used to be this banner's Common and the starter hero. He is now its Mythical, and **Gide** is the Common and the starter: a new Master begins with Gide in squad slot 1 (locked) and Catio on the bench. Hans summoned before the rework keep their saved Common rarity and stats, and only take the new look. Old-save migrations pin Han to Common for the same reason (`ProfileMigrations.LEGACY_RARITY`). His base stats and kit are unchanged; only the rarity multiplier differs.

#### 2. Limited Time Banner: "A Star's Arrival" (Rarity Tiers 1–7)
The inaugural limited-time rate-up banner features exactly **one character for each rarity from Common to Mythical** (7 total heroes):
- **Summon Cost:** `200 Gems` per single pull (`1,800 Gems` for 10x pull).
- **Pity System:** Guaranteed **Exalted or higher** hero within **100 summons** (`MaxPity = 100`, `PityMinTier = 5`). The pity pull is split by the banner's Exalted/Legendary/Mythical rates (with God's Blessing luck): about 77% Exalted (Archon or Valeria), 19% Legendary (Solaris or Rhyman, evenly) and 4% Astrea. Any natural Exalted+ pull resets the counter.
- **50/50 rule:** whenever an Exalted+ hero that is *not* Astrea is pulled on this banner (natural or pity), the next pity is guaranteed to be **Astrea** (`HeroLimitedFeaturedGuarantee`). Pulling Astrea clears the guarantee. The summon screen shows "Guaranteed Astrea in N summons" while it is active. Worst case for Astrea: 200 summons.
- **10-Pull Guarantee:** Every 10x pull includes at least one **Rare** or higher hero.
- **Drop Rates:**
  - `Common (61.14%)`: **Jenna**
  - `Uncommon (24.0%)`: **Velkist**
  - `Rare (9.5%)`: **Eolka**
  - `Superior (3.8%)`: **Morrigan**
  - `Exalted (1.2%)`: **Sovereign Archon** or **Valeria**
  - `Legendary (0.30%)`: **Solaris** or **Rhyman** (0.15% each)
  - `Mythical (0.06%)`: **Astrea (Featured Starlight Mythical)**

*(The relic economy rebalance raised Gem income about 1.3–1.5×, so both banners were tightened to keep hours of play per hero at or above the old pace: Call of the Rift from 70/20/7/2.5/0.5% with pity 80, and A Star's Arrival from 60/24/10/4.2/1.4/0.33/0.07% with pity 90 and a 100-Gem price. See `docs/relic-loot-rebalance-plan.md`. Earlier history: `docs/rework-plan/PHASE_4_ONBOARDING_AND_PROGRESSION.md` §7.)*

#### 3. Premium Banner: "The Gilded Mirror" (Rarity Tiers 7–11, Robux)
Five **mirror heroes** (`HeroCatalog.MirrorBanner`, plan: `docs/gilded-mirror-plan.md`). Every pull is one of the five; there is no filler.
- **Drop Rates:** `Mythical (50%)`: **Isabelle** · `Transcendent (28%)`: **Nyx** · `Ascendant (14%)`: **Caspian** · `Ethereal (6%)`: **Seraphine** · `Immortal (2%)`: **Rook (Featured)**.
- **Robux only** (`ShopConfig.Mirror`, granted by `ShopService` through `DataManager.RollMirrorSummons`): **55 R$** for one pull, **499 R$** for ten. There is no Gem price, no God's Blessing luck and no pity counter; Gem summons on this banner are refused. The Store shows the five heroes with their mirror share and the full odds before purchase.
- **10-Pull Guarantee:** every ten-pull includes **Caspian or better** (`TenPullMinTier = 9`).
- **Spark:** every pull adds to `profile.MirrorSpark.Pulls`; each **80 pulls** bank one free pick of any of the five (`Selections`), spent in the Store through `ProgressionRemotes.ClaimMirrorSpark` (`Util/MirrorBanner`). Older saves gain the counter lazily in `SyncProfile`.
- **Paid random items:** players whose `PolicyService` restricts them never see the banner; a purchase that still arrives becomes **150 Gems per pull** (the Gem store's rate).
- **Live once ProductIds are set:** both offers have `ProductId = 0` (COMING SOON). In Studio, the chat command `/mirrorpull [1|10]` runs the pulls without a purchase.
- **Mirror heroes have no stats of their own.** Their base power is a share of the Master's **best normal hero** (highest power without a weapon or Training Grounds; mirror heroes never copy each other): **110 / 120 / 130 / 140 / 150%** for rarity 7–11, **+2% per star** above 1★ (`StatBalance.MirrorBase`, `MirrorPerStar`). A 7★ Isabelle (122%) beats a 1★ Nyx (120%). The share is split into HP and ATK by the mirror hero's **own** archetype shape (its seed HP, ATK and interval), so $10 	imes DPS + HP$ before the weapon equals the target. Their own weapon and Training Grounds then apply as usual, and Rift Attunement still caps them (§2.5). Labor copies the source's labor at 100%, with the mirror hero's own facility synergy (`StatFormulas.GetMirrorBase`, `RefreshMirrorSources`).
- **UI:** hero lists show "Mirror 152%" where other heroes show a level (`StatFormulas.LevelText`), the Backpack says which hero it copies (`MirrorDescription`), the Hero Index has a Gilded Mirror banner (its Summon button opens the Store), and the Promotion tab shows a Mirror row, the hero being copied and a reminder that promoting that hero lifts every mirror hero ×1.5. Rarity 8–11 have their own colours (teal, orchid, indigo, rose).
- **In the world** (`Rendering/MirrorHeroFx`): a mirror hero draws a gold thread to the hero it copies when it appears and every 14 s, and throws off a small gold glint every 6–10 s (the glint stops under Reduce Motion).
- **Every roster keeps one normal hero.** The Pit refuses to consume a Master's last normal hero as a donor, alone or together with others (§4.2a).

---

#### Inherent Hero Rarity
Like weapons (Section 7.1), every hero template has a fixed **inherent rarity**. For heroes it sets a stat multiplier; every hero is summoned at 1★.
- **Rarity** uses the weapon tier names. Launch heroes span tiers 1 (Common) to 7 (Mythical); the Gilded Mirror heroes use 7–11 (Transcendent, Ascendant, Ethereal, Immortal), and tiers 12–16 are reserved. Rarity can never be upgraded, rerolled or refined. For a mirror hero, rarity only sets its mirror share; the multipliers below don't apply to it.
- **Rarity multipliers:** heroes used to be summoned at a star equal to their tier, which gave tier $t$ an extra $3^{(t-1)}$ combat and $1.35^{(t-1)}$ labor. That bonus now lives in the rarity multiplier, scaled by a compensation factor $c_t$:
  $$R_{\text{combat}}(t) = c_t \times (3 \times 1.35)^{(t-1)} \qquad R_{\text{labor}}(t) = c_t \times (1.35 \times 1.12)^{(t-1)}$$
  where $c_t = 1$ for Common, Uncommon and Rare (they keep 100% of their old summoned power, so the early floors stay easy) and $c_t = 0.5$ from Superior up (`StatBalance.RarityStarCompensation`). A fresh Superior (×33.2) still beats a fresh Rare (×16.4).
- **Stars:** every hero is summoned at 1★ (`StatBalance.HeroSummonStar`) and promotes at The Pit up to 7★ (`PitBalanceConfig.MaxStars`), at ×1.5 combat per star. Rarity never changes when a hero is promoted.
- **The Pit** values a donor at its *effective star*, Stars + Rarity − 1 (capped at 7), for synthesis XP and the rare-donor confirmation, so a 1★ Mythical still feeds like the 7★ it used to arrive as.
- **Existing saves (schema v4)** drop the stars a hero got for free at summon ($t - 1$) and keep any promotions bought on top. Levels above the new cap ($Stars \times 10$) are clamped. **Schema v5** converts the absolute level to the per-star level $L - (S-1) \times 10$, clamped to 1–10.

#### Launch Roster Base & Native Stats
Every summoned hero rolls a `QualityScalar` $q \in [0.90, 1.15]$ applied to their archetype base stats. Native stats are the stats at the summoned **1★, Lv. 1**: $Stat_0 \times R_{\text{combat}}(t)$ for combat and $L_0 \times R_{\text{labor}}(t)$ for labor (without facility synergy):
